- **5d-build**: Execute implementation tasks with quality and awareness. - **5d-gap-analysis**: Analyze the gap between current state and specification to identify all required changes. Use when: (1) After SPEC phase in 5D-SDD workflow, (2) Spec exists and implementation is about to begin, (3) User asks 'what needs to change' or 'what's the delta,' (4) Assessing scope of work. This phase prevents surprise discoveries during implementation. ([tapania/5d-skills](https://github.com/tapania/5d-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/5d-gap-analysis) · ★5)
- **5d-orient**: Pre-development orientation and assumption mapping for spec-driven development. Use when: (1) Starting a new feature or project, (2) User says 'let's build X' without prior analysis, (3) Beginning any 5D-SDD workflow, (4) User wants to 'think through' an idea before planning. This phase prevents wasted effort by surfacing hidden assumptions and identifying all relevant domains before committing to a direction. ([tapania/5d-skills](https://github.com/tapania/5d-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/5d-orient) · ★5)
- **5d-plan**: Convert expanded thinking into a concrete, multi-perspective plan. Use when: (1) After SPAR phase in 5D-SDD workflow, (2) User is ready to define what will be built, (3) User asks to 'write up the plan' or 'document what we're building,' (4) Transitioning from exploration to commitment. This phase creates the authoritative description of intent before technical specification. ([tapania/5d-skills](https://github.com/tapania/5d-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/5d-plan) · ★5)
- **5d-refine**: Stress-test the plan through adversarial review and edge case analysis. Use when: (1) After PLAN phase in 5D-SDD workflow, (2) User wants to 'validate,' 'review,' or 'check' a plan, (3) Before committing significant resources to implementation, (4) Plan exists but hasn't been challenged. This phase catches plan-level errors before they become code-level errors. ([tapania/5d-skills](https://github.com/tapania/5d-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/5d-refine) · ★5)
- **5d-reflect**: Extract learning from completed work to improve future cycles.
